adopt a new or amended plan.
137D Attendance management plan to be on Internet site
A board must ensure that its attendance management plan is available to the public on an Internet site maintained by or on behalf of the board.
 Strategic planning and reporting
138 School strategic plan and annual implementation plan
A board must have the following strategic planning documents for its school:
a strategic plan, for each 3-year period or for a shorter period determined by the Secretary, that sets out the board’s strategy for achieving (or making progress towards achieving) its objectives during that period; and
an annual implementation plan for each year that sets out how the board intends to implement that strategy during the year.
A board must prepare its first strategic plan and annual implementation plan when required by regulations made under section 639 to do so.
If, at the commencement of this section, a board has a charter in effect for the 2022 year, the charter is to be treated as the board’s first strategic plan.
If a board’s strategic plan is its 2022 school charter, the statement of variance is not required to include a comparison with an annual implementation plan.
However, a board with a 2022 school charter as its strategic plan must continue to update the annually updated sections of its charter until its first annual implementation plan is required under regulations made under section 639.
The annually updated sections of a 2022 school charter must be updated no later than a date fixed by the Secretary.
139 Preparing strategic plan
A board must prepare a strategic plan for every 3-year period, or for a shorter period determined by the Secretary, and submit it to the Secretary in accordance with regulations made under section 639.
The strategic plan must comply with any regulations made under section 639 relating to the form and content of strategic plans.
In preparing a strategic plan, the board must—
consult—
the school community; and
the school’s staff; and
where appropriate, the school’s students; and
any other persons required by the regulations; and
comply with any other regulations relating to the development of strategic plans; and
in the case of a State integrated school, ensure that the plan reflects the school’s special character; and
in the case of a designated character school, ensure that the plan reflects the school’s different character or, in the case of a Kura Kaupapa Māori, its special characteristics.
In preparing a strategic plan, the board may consult any person, group, or organisation that it thinks fit to consult.
This section is subject to section 138(3).
140 Secretary may review strategic plan
The Secretary may review a strategic plan in accordance with regulations made under section 639.
After reviewing the strategic plan, the Secretary may direct that the board—
consider, or further consider, any matter and revise the plan in the light of that consideration; or
revise the plan as directed by the Secretary.
If the strategic plan is returned to the board, the board must comply with the Secretary’s directions and then resubmit a revised plan.
If a board fails to comply with the Secretary’s directions, or resubmits a revised strategic plan that still does not meet the requirements of the Act or the regulations, the Secretary may revise the plan and confirm it by giving written notice to the board.
141 Amending strategic plan
A board may amend its strategic plan.
If the board considers that a proposed amendment to its strategic plan is significant, the board must comply with the requirements of section 139(3) before confirming an amendment.
The Secretary may require a board to amend its strategic plan.
142 Expiry of strategic plan
A strategic plan expires 3 years after the plan takes effect.
However, a 2022 school charter (which is a document that is to be treated as a board’s first strategic plan) expires when replaced by a strategic plan adopted in accordance with regulations made under section 639.
If there is no new plan to replace the expired plan, the expired plan continues to apply for a period approved by the Secretary.
143 Preparing annual implementation plan
A board must prepare an annual implementation plan.
The plan must—
contain the information required by regulations made under section 639; and
be prepared in accordance with those regulations.
144 Amending annual implementation plan
A board may amend its annual implementation plan.
145 Board to monitor performance against strategic planning documents
A board must monitor and evaluate its performance—
in achieving (or making progress towards achieving) its objectives in accordance with its strategic plan; and
in implementing its strategy in accordance with its annual implementation plan.
The monitoring and evaluation must be carried out in accordance with regulations made under section 639.
The board must report on its performance in the annual report, in accordance with regulations made under section 639.
146 Strategic planning documents to be on Internet site
A board must ensure that its strategic plan and annual implementation plan are available to the public on an Internet site maintained by or on behalf of the board.
 Validity of acts and liability
147 Acts in breach of statute invalid
An act of a board is invalid (unless section 148 applies) if—
the act is contrary to, or outside the authority of, an Act; or
the act is done otherwise than for the purpose of performing its functions.
Subsection (1) does not limit any discretion of a court to grant relief in respect of a minor or technical breach.
148 Some natural person acts protected
Section 147, or any rule of law to similar effect, does not prevent a person dealing with a board from enforcing a transaction that is a natural person act unless the person dealing with the board knew, or ought reasonably to have known, that—
an express restriction in an Act makes the act contrary to, or outside the authority of, the Act; or
the act was done otherwise than for the purpose of performing the board’s functions.
A person who relies on subsection (1) has the onus of proving that the person did not have, and could not reasonably be expected to have had, the knowledge referred to in that subsection.
A board must report, in its annual report, each transaction that the board has performed in the year to which the report relates that was invalid under section 147 but enforced in reliance on subsection (1).
To avoid doubt, this section does not affect any person’s remedies under the general law (for example, remedies in contract).
In this section, natural person act—
means an act that a natural person of full age and capacity can do (whether or not the act is something that is also authorised by an Act); and
includes entry into a contract for, or relating to,—
the acquisition of financial products (within the meaning of section 7 of the Financial Markets Conduct Act 2013) or the borrowing of money; or
the purchase, leasing, or sale of, or other dealings with, property; or
the employment, or engagement of the services, of a person.
149 Acts that are not in best interests of board
It is irrelevant to the validity of an act that the act is not, or may not be, in the best interests of a board.
150 Dealings between boards and other persons
A board may not assert against a person dealing with the board that—
a person held out by the board to be a member, office holder, chief executive, employee, or agent of the board—
has not been duly appointed in that capacity or has ceased to be appointed in that capacity; or
does not have the authority to exercise a power that, given the nature of the board, a person appointed to that capacity customarily has the authority to exercise; or
does not have the authority to exercise a power that the board holds the person out as having; or
a document issued on behalf of the board by a member, office holder, chief executive, employee, or agent of the board who has actual or usual authority to issue the document is not valid or genuine.
However, a board may assert any of those matters if the person dealing with the board has, or could reasonably be expected to have had, knowledge of the matter.
Nothing in this section affects a person’s right to apply, in accordance with the law, for judicial review.
151 Personal liability of board members
A board member is not personally liable for—
any act done or omitted by the board; or
any loss to the board arising out of any act done or omitted by the board member.
However, subsection (1) applies only if the act or omission was (as far as the board member’s involvement is concerned) in good faith in the performance or intended performance of the functions of the board.
 Financial and property matters and application of Crown Entities Act 2004
152 Board to be financially responsible
A board must perform its functions and exercise its powers in a way that is financially responsible.
153 Application of Crown Entities Act 2004
A board is a Crown entity for the purposes of section 7 of the Crown Entities Act 2004.
However, that Act applies to boards only to the extent that subsection (3) provides.
The provisions set out in Schedule 3 of the Crown Entities Act 2004 apply to boards and their Crown entity subsidiaries (within the meaning of that Act).
A board must comply with any direction given under section 107 of the Crown Entities Act 2004.
If the board does not comply with a direction, it may be dismissed under section 171(1)﻿(i).
154 Restrictions on acquisition of securities
Sections 160 and 161 of the Crown Entities Act 2004 apply.
Under section 161 of the Crown Entities Act 2004, a board may not acquire securities—
other than—
a debt security denominated in New Zealand dollars that is issued by a registered bank, or by any other entity, that satisfies a credit-rating test that is specified in regulations made under Part 4 of the Crown Entities Act 2004 or a notice in the Gazette published by the Minister of Finance; or
a public security; or
otherwise than as provided in—
any regulations made under Part 4 of that Act; or
any approval given jointly by the Minister of Education and the Minister of Finance; or
this Act.
155 Restrictions on borrowing
Sections 160 and 162 of the Crown Entities Act 2004 apply.
Under sections 160 and 162 of that Act, a board may not borrow from any person, or amend the terms of any borrowing, otherwise than as provided in—
any regulations made under Part 4 of that Act; or
any approval given jointly by the Minister of Education and the Minister of Finance; or
this Act.
156 No delegation of power to borrow
A board may not delegate any power to borrow money that it may have under section 160 or 162 of the Crown Entities Act 2004.
157 Restrictions on giving of guarantees and indemnities
Sections 160 and 163 of the Crown Entities Act 2004 apply.
Under sections 160 and 163 of that Act, a board may not, with or without security, give a guarantee to, or indemnify, another person otherwise than as provided in—
any regulations made under Part 4 of that Act; or
any approval given jointly by the Minister of Education and the Minister of Finance; or
this Act.
158 Restrictions on use of derivatives
Sections 160 and 164 of the Crown Entities Act 2004 apply.
Under sections 160 and 164 of that Act, a board may not enter into an agreement constituting a derivative, or amend the terms of that agreement, otherwise than as provided in—
any regulations made under Part 4 of that Act; or
any approval given jointly by the Minister of Education and the Minister of Finance; or
this Act.
159 Gifts
Any money or property that is gifted to a school may be accepted or disclaimed by the board in accordance with section 167 of the Crown Entities Act 2004.
A limitation that is provided in this Act or that applies under the Crown Entities Act 2004 (for example, a limitation on the form in which property may be held) does not apply during a period that is reasonable in the circumstances.
Subsections (1) and (2) apply to any gift that is received by the board for funding scholarships or bursaries, or for other educational purposes in connection with a school.
A board must hold the gift for the specific purpose declared by the giver.
Unless the giver has created a special trust, scholarships and bursaries from a gift must be open to every student at the school.
If the school for which a gift was given closes, the Minister must direct that the gift should apply to another school.
160 Real property
Except as provided in section 159, a board may not acquire an interest in land, or any licence to occupy any land or premises, without the consent of the Minister.
161 Occupancy of property and buildings
The Board of the NZSPA may—
specify terms and conditions applying generally to land and buildings occupied by boards:
by written notice to a particular board, specify terms and conditions applying to land and buildings occupied by the board.
Before specifying any terms and conditions under this section, the Board of the NZSPA must consult the Secretary.
Terms and conditions under subsection (1)—
may apply to any land and buildings occupied by a board (regardless of who owns the property); but
to the extent that it applies to the board of a State integrated school, is subject to Schedule 6 and to the integration agreement for the time being in force between the Minister and the proprietor of the school.
Terms and conditions under subsection (1) may include standards of maintenance, standards of capital works, and minimum safety and health requirements.
Subsection (3) does not limit the generality of subsection (1).
Terms and conditions may be specified under subsection (1) for—
a particular school or institution or particular schools or institutions; or
schools or institutions of particular classes or descriptions; or
all schools and institutions.
Terms and conditions under subsection (1)﻿(a) are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
Terms and conditions specified under subsection (1) apply to boards or a board as if—
the land and buildings were owned by the Crown and the Crown has leased them to the board; and
the terms and conditions were part of the lease; and
the Crown had empowered the Board of the NZSPA to exercise the Crown’s powers concerning the lease.
162 Leases and licences granted by boards
A board may, with the written consent of the Board of the NZSPA, grant a lease or a licence to occupy to any person in respect of any land, buildings, or facilities occupied by the board.
The Board of the NZSPA may agree to the grant of a lease or a licence by the board only if satisfied that—
the land, building, or facilities are not needed or used for the purposes of the school during the time covered by the lease or licence; and
the lease or licence is in the public interest; and
the lease or licence—
is for a purpose associated with educational outcomes and is to bring educational benefit to the school or its community, or to any other school; or
is for a community purpose, and has no educational disadvantage for the school.
The Board of the NZSPA must determine the terms and conditions of any lease or licence granted by a board by doing either or both of the following:
specifying the general terms and conditions that apply to all, or specified classes of, leases or licences:
giving written notice to the board.
Terms and conditions specified under subsection (3)﻿(a) are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
In relation to a State integrated school, this section applies subject to Schedule 6 and to any integration agreement in force between the Minister and the proprietor of the school.
163 Other agreements to occupy school land or buildings
A board may not enter into an agreement unless the agreement—
is of a type permitted by notice under subsection (4); and
is consistent with this section and any conditions specified by notice under subsection (4).
It is a condition of every agreement that the board has the right to enter, at any time, the land, buildings, or facilities that are the subject of the agreement.
No person has the right under an agreement to use or occupy any land, buildings, or facilities in such a way as to unduly interfere with the use, by the board for school purposes, of that land or those buildings or facilities, or any other land, buildings, or facilities of the school.
The Board of the NZSPA may, by notice,—
identify the kinds of agreements (for example, agreements for the use of playing fields) that boards may enter into; and
specify conditions to which agreements, or specified types of agreements, are subject.
A notice under subsection (4) is secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
In relation to a State integrated school, this section applies subject to Schedule 6 and to any integration agreement in force between the Minister and the proprietor of the school.
In this section, agreement means an agreement, other than a lease or a licence to occupy under section 162, between a board and any other person for the use of land, buildings, or facilities occupied by the board.
 Programmes and monitoring
164 Teaching and learning programmes
The board of a school must ensure that the school’s principal and staff develop and implement teaching and learning programmes that—
give effect to any national curriculum statements made under section 90; and
give the school’s students access to a nationally and internationally recognised qualifications system.
165 Monitoring of and reporting on student performance
The board of a school must ensure that the school’s principal and staff monitor and evaluate the performance of the school’s students.
Monitoring and evaluating must include, but is not limited to, monitoring and evaluating the performance of the students in relation to—
any national curriculum statements made under section 90; and
any qualifications system referred to in section 164(b) that is offered at the school.
The board must ensure that information about a student’s performance is given to a parent of the student in a timely manner and in a form that is readily understandable.
The board must report to the Secretary, to its school community, and to parents on the performance of the school’s students in accordance with any regulations made under section 639.
 Code of conduct
166 Code of conduct for board members
The Minister may issue a code of conduct for the members of State school boards that—
sets out the minimum standards of conduct that each member is required to meet; and
is consistent with any code issued under section 598.
Before issuing a code of conduct for the members of State school boards, the Minister—
must consult the national bodies representing the interests of boards; and
may consult any other persons or bodies that the Minister thinks fit.
A code of conduct under this section—
is secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements); and
comes into force on a date specified in it, being not less than 28 days after it is published under that Act.
167 Boards may augment code of conduct for board members
A board, by resolution, may specify additional standards of conduct for its members provided they are consistent with any code issued under section 166 and the requirements under this Act and any other enactment.
168 Board members must comply with code of conduct
Every board member must comply with any code issued under section 166.
However, if a conflict between a standard in a code issued under section 166 (or in a code augmented under section 167) and a standard in a code issued under section 485 arises, the standard in the code issued under section 485 prevails.
169 Sanctions for failures to comply with code of conduct
A board may, by resolution, censure a board member (other than a principal) for any significant or persistent breach of a code issued under section 166 (or a code augmented under section 167).
The Minister may remove a board member (other than a principal) if—
the board member has significantly or persistently breached a code issued under section 166 (or a code augmented under section 167); and
the board—
considers the board member’s failure to comply with the code warrants the removal of the board member; and
has presented the Minister with a written report about the board member’s failure that recommends the removal of the board member; and
the Minister is satisfied that there is just cause to remove the board member.
The removal must be made by written notice to the board member (with a copy to the board).
The notice must state—
the day on which the removal takes effect, which must be no earlier than the day on which the notice is received; and
the reasons for the removal.
The Minister must notify the removal in the Gazette as soon as practicable after giving the notice.
The Minister may remove a board member with as little formality and technicality, and as much expedition, as is permitted by—
the principles of natural justice; and
a proper consideration of the matter; and
the requirements of this section.
In this section, just cause includes misconduct, inability to perform the functions of office, neglect of duty, and breach of any of the collective duties of board members or the individual duties of members (depending on the seriousness of the breach).
 Validation
170 Validation and invalidation of board elections
Subsection (2) applies if—
anything required to be done in connection with an election under this Act—
has been done after the time it is required to be done; or
has not been done at all; or
has been done irregularly; and
the Minister thinks the lateness, omission, or irregularity could not materially have affected the result of the election.
If this subsection applies, the Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, validate the lateness, omission, or irregularity.
Where anything required to be done in connection with an election under this Act cannot be done at or by the time at or by which it is required to be done, the Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, extend the time for doing it.
Subsection (5) applies if the following occurs in connection with an election under this Act:
any lateness, omission, or irregularity that is capable of being validated under this section, but that the Minister thinks it would be improper or undesirable to validate; or
any other irregularity that the Minister thinks could materially have affected the result of the election.
If this subsection applies, the Minister may within 60 days of the election, by notice in the Gazette,—
declare the election invalid; and
require a new election to be held on a day specified in the notice; and
either—
declare that the board members holding office on the date of the invalid election remain in office until the close of the day before the day on which the new board members take office; or
direct the Secretary to appoint a commissioner to act in place of the board until the close of the day before the day on which the new board members take office.
 State schools of serious concern
170A Notification by Chief Review Officer
This section applies if the Chief Review Officer forms a view, following the performance of 1 or more of their functions under section 463, that a State school may be of serious concern.
The Chief Review Officer must notify the Secretary and the Minister of their view within 2 working days after forming that view.
170B Report by Chief Review Officer
The Chief Review Officer must give the Secretary and the Minister a written report—
stating whether the school notified under section 170A is of serious concern to the Chief Review Officer; and
recommending 1 or more interventions under section 171 that may address the matter (if the report states that the school is of serious concern to the Chief Review Officer).
The report must be given within 28 working days after the Secretary and the Minister receive the notification under section 170A.
170C Report by Secretary
This section applies if the Secretary receives a report under section 170B stating that a school is of serious concern to the Chief Review Officer.
The Secretary must give the Chief Review Officer and the Minister a written report that sets out—
what action (if any) the Secretary has taken in relation to the school; or
what action the Secretary intends to take in relation to the school, and the time frame for that action; or
what action the Secretary recommends that the Minister take in relation to the school, with reasons for the recommendation.
The report must be given within 30 working days after the Secretary receives the report under section 170B.
 Interventions in State schools
171 Interventions in State schools by Secretary or Minister
The following interventions may be used in relation to a State school:
a requirement by the Secretary for information:
a requirement by the Secretary for a board to engage specialist help:
a requirement by the Secretary for a board to prepare and carry out an action plan:
a requirement by the Secretary that the board attend a case conference to enable a particular issue or particular issues to be discussed and actions to be agreed:
a requirement by the Secretary that the board engage an appropriately qualified person to undertake a specialist audit of any aspect of the school’s affairs:
the issuing by the Secretary to the board of a performance notice requiring the board to carry out a specified action by a specified date:
the appointment by the Minister of a board member (who may be the presiding board member) to the board for a specified period of time:
the appointment by the Secretary, at the direction of the Minister, of a limited statutory manager:
the dissolution of a board by the Minister and the appointment of a commissioner:
the dissolution of a board by the Secretary and the appointment of a commissioner in accordance with section 181(3).
The Secretary may use an intervention described in subsection (1)﻿(a) to (f) only if the Secretary has reasonable grounds for concern about the operation of the school or the welfare or educational performance of its students.
The Secretary or the Minister may use an intervention described in subsection (1)﻿(g) to (i) in relation to a school only if the Secretary or the Minister has reasonable grounds to believe that there is a risk to the operation of the school or to the welfare or educational performance of its students.
In considering whether reasonable grounds are established under subsection (2) or (3), the Minister or the Secretary may rely on and take into account information in a report given under section 170B.
Subsection (3A) does not limit the information that the Minister or the Secretary can rely on or take into account for the purposes of subsection (2) or (3).
The Secretary or the Minister may use an intervention described in subsection (1) in relation to a school if either of the following requests the intervention:
the board:
for a State integrated school, the school's proprietors.
When applying an intervention, the Secretary or the Minister must apply whichever intervention they consider is reasonable to deal with the risk without intervening more than necessary in the affairs of the school.
The application of any one intervention does not preclude applying any other intervention, either concurrently or at any other time.
172 Requirement to provide information
The Secretary may, by written notice to a board, require the board to provide specified information—
as at a given time or times; or
at specified intervals; or
both.
The Secretary may also require, in the notice, that an analysis of the specified information be provided.
A board that receives a notice under subsection (1) must provide the Secretary with the information required and an analysis of the information (if that has been sought)—
by the time or times, or at the intervals, or both, as specified in the notice; and
in the form (if any) required by the Secretary.
173 Specialist help
The Secretary may, by written notice to a board, require the board to engage specified specialist help.
A notice given under subsection (1) must identify particular persons or organisations, or types of persons or organisations, whom the board must engage.
The Secretary may also require, in the notice, that the board provide to the Secretary a report or reports (for example, a progress report and a final report) on the specialist help—
by the specified time or times; or
at the specified intervals; or
both.
A board that receives a notice under subsection (1) must—
engage the specialist help as soon as practicable; and
pay the fees and reasonable expenses of any person or organisation engaged to provide specialist help, unless the Secretary determines otherwise; and
provide a report or reports to the Secretary at the time or times, or intervals, specified in the notice.
174 Action plans
The Secretary may, by written notice to a board, require the board to prepare and carry out an action plan, and any notice must specify—
the matters that the action plan must address; and
the outcomes sought; and
the time within which a draft action plan must be prepared.
A board that receives a notice under subsection (1) must comply with it by preparing a draft action plan within the time specified in the notice and presenting it to the Secretary for approval.
The Secretary may negotiate with the board over the draft action plan in order to reach an agreed plan, but if, after a reasonable period, the board and the Secretary have not reached agreement over the content of the action plan, the Secretary may give notice to the board that the Secretary is to approve a particular version of the plan.
When the Secretary has approved an action plan, the board must—
implement it in accordance with its terms, unless or until the Secretary directs otherwise; and
make the plan available as if it were part of the strategic plan required under section 138.
175 Case conference
The Secretary may, by written notice to a board, require the board to attend a case conference on a specified date.
The notice must specify the issue or issues to be discussed.
A board that receives a notice under subsection (1) must attend the case conference.
The Secretary may invite any person to attend the case conference if the Secretary considers that the person’s presence at the conference is desirable.
If the case conference results in the parties reaching agreement on any action or actions to address any issue or issues, the agreement—
must be recorded in writing; and
is binding on the parties.
If the Secretary and the board are not able to agree on an action or actions to address any issue or issues, the Secretary may, by written notice to the board, require it—
to take a particular action or particular actions; and
to provide the Secretary with a report or reports (for example, a progress report and a final report) on the action or actions taken—
as at a given time or given times; or
at specified intervals; or
both.
A board that receives a notice under subsection (6) must,—
in relation to a requirement to take a particular action or particular actions, take the action or actions as soon as practicable; and
if the notice includes a requirement to report to the Secretary, provide a report or reports at the time or times, or intervals, specified in the notice.
176 Specialist audit
The Secretary may, by written notice to the board, require a board to engage an appropriately qualified person to undertake a specialist audit of any aspect of the school’s affairs.
A notice given under subsection (1) must identify particular persons or organisations, or types of persons or organisations, whom the board must engage.
The Secretary may also require, in the notice, that the board provide the Secretary with a report or reports (for example, a progress report and a final report) on the audit—
as at a given time or times; or
at specified intervals; or
both.
A board that receives a notice under subsection (1) must—
undertake the audit as soon as practicable; and
pay the fees and reasonable expenses of any person or organisation engaged to undertake the audit, unless the Secretary determines otherwise; and
provide a report or reports to the Secretary at the time or times, or intervals, specified in the notice.
177 Performance notice
The Secretary may, by written notice to a board, issue a performance notice requiring the board to carry out a specified action by a specified date.
The Secretary may also require, in the notice, that the board provide the Secretary with a report or reports (for example, a progress report and a final report) on the action taken—
as at a given time or given times; or
at specified intervals; or
both.
A board that receives a notice under subsection (1) must—
take the action by the date specified in the notice; and
provide a report or reports to the Secretary at the time or times, or intervals, specified in the notice.
178 Appointment of additional board member by Minister
The Minister may, by written notice to a board, appoint an additional board member, and the Minister may also appoint that board member as the presiding board member.
The notice must specify a period of time for which the appointment is made.
A person who is ineligible to be a board member under this Act or any regulations made under this Act may not be appointed under this section.
179 Amendment and revocation of notices
The Secretary may, by giving written notice to a board, amend or revoke a notice given by the Secretary under any of sections 172 to 177, 180, and 181.
The amendment or revocation takes effect on the date specified in the notice.
180 Limited statutory manager
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, direct the Secretary to appoint a limited statutory manager for a board.
A notice under subsection (1) must specify—
any functions, powers, and duties of the board (whether statutory or otherwise) that are to be vested in the limited statutory manager; and
any matters on which the limited statutory manager may or must advise the board; and
any conditions attaching to the exercise of the powers by the board or by the limited statutory manager.
On publication of the Gazette notice, the Secretary must, by notice to the board,—
appoint a person to be the limited statutory manager for the board; and
state the date on which the appointment takes effect.
A notice under subsection (3) appointing a person to be the limited statutory manager for the board may refer to the person appointed—
by the limited statutory manager’s own name; and
as being of a named body corporate.
On and from the date on which the limited statutory manager’s appointment takes effect,—
any functions, powers, or duties of the board specified in a notice under subsection (1) vest in the limited statutory manager; and
the board must take into consideration advice given by the limited statutory manager on any matter on which the manager is obliged to give advice; and
any conditions specified in the notice apply.
The board must pay the fees and expenses of a limited statutory manager appointed for it, unless the Secretary determines otherwise.
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, amend a notice under subsection (1), and the amendment takes effect on and from the date given in the notice.
If the Minister is satisfied that the appointment of the limited statutory manager is no longer required, the Minister must revoke the notice under subsection (1), in which case the appointment terminates from the date of the revocation.
181 Dissolution of board and appointment of commissioner
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, dissolve a board and direct the Secretary to appoint a commissioner to replace that board.
On publication of a notice under subsection (1), the Secretary must, by notice in the Gazette, appoint a commissioner for the school and state the date on which the appointment takes effect.
The Secretary may, by notice in the Gazette, dissolve the board and appoint a commissioner in its place, as from a specified date, if any of the following applies:
the board has not held a meeting during the previous 3 months:
so many casual vacancies have arisen that there is no longer any board member who is eligible to preside at meetings of the board:
the result of an election of board members is that the board has fewer than 3 board members elected by parents:
an election of board members has not been held as required by this Act:
it is impossible or impracticable to discover the results of an election of board members:
the Minister has directed the Secretary to appoint a commissioner under subsection (1).
A notice under subsection (2) or (3) appointing a commissioner for a school may refer to the person appointed—
by the commissioner’s own name; and
as being of a named body corporate.
182 No compensation for loss of office
A board member is not entitled to any compensation or other payment or benefit relating to the board member ceasing for any reason to hold office as a board member.
A board must ensure, to the extent that it is reasonably able to do so, that each of its Crown entity subsidiaries does not pay directors of the subsidiary any compensation or other payment or benefit, on any basis, for ceasing for any reason to hold office.
However, subsections (4) and (5) apply to a person who is entitled, at the commencement of this section under any contract or arrangement, to any compensation or other payment or benefit relating to their ceasing for any reason to hold office.
The entitlement is not affected by the enactment of this section.
The entitlement is cancelled on the date of reappointment of the board member or director or of the member to the committee (if any).
183 Commissioners
A commissioner appointed under section 170(5)﻿(c)﻿(ii) or 181 has all the functions, duties, and powers of the board that the commissioner is appointed to replace.
Anything that, if done by or on behalf of the board, must be done by affixing the board's seal, or by the signature of 2 or more board members, or both, may be done by the signature of the commissioner.
The remuneration of the commissioner must be determined by the Secretary and paid for out of funds of the board, unless the Secretary determines otherwise.
184 Commissioner sets date for election of board members
In the case of a commissioner appointed under section 181(2), when the Secretary is satisfied that a commissioner is no longer required for the school, the commissioner must appoint a date for the election of board members.
In the case of a commissioner appointed under section 181(3), when the Secretary is satisfied that an election of board members is likely to produce a functioning board, the commissioner must appoint a date for the election of board members.
A commissioner's appointment ends 7 days after the date that the commissioner has set for the election of board members.
185 Personal liability of limited statutory managers and commissioners
A limited statutory manager or commissioner is not personally liable for any act done or omitted by the manager or commissioner, or for any loss arising out of any act done or omitted by them, if the act or omission was in good faith and occurred in the course of performing their functions.
186 Annual review of interventions
Within 1 year of the date of a notice under any of sections 172 to 178, 180, and 181, the Secretary must review the operation of the intervention commenced by each notice, and after that must review the operation of the intervention annually.
187 Application of interventions to State integrated schools
The Secretary must, if practicable, consult with the proprietors of a State integrated school before appointing a limited statutory manager or commissioner for the school, and must have regard to any recommendations made by the proprietors.
If the Secretary considers that it is not practicable to consult with the proprietors before making an appointment, the Secretary must consult with the proprietors after making the appointment and must consider whether, in light of any recommendations made by the proprietors, a different person should be appointed in place of the original appointee.
188 Application of interventions to Kura Kaupapa Māori
Before applying any of the interventions in this subpart to a Kura Kaupapa Māori, the Secretary must consult with te kaitiaki o Te Aho Matua.
Subsection (1) applies only to Kura Kaupapa Māori that are required by their school charters to operate in accordance with Te Aho Matua.
 Subpart 6 — Establishment and designation of State schools
189 Overview: classifications and types of State schools
This subpart concerns—
the following classifications of State schools:
primary school:
intermediate school:
secondary school:
composite school:
the following types of State schools:
ordinary State school:
designated character school:
Kura Kaupapa Māori:
State integrated school:
specialist school:
distance school.
190 Minister may establish State schools
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, establish a State school by—
describing the place where the school is to be located; and
for a secondary school, specifying whether it is a boys' school, a girls' school, or a co-educational school; and
specifying a name for the school.
A decision to establish a school under subsection (1) is in the Minister’s absolute discretion.
Despite subsection (2), the Minister must establish a State school if required to do so under section 212ZEB.
A school established under this section must be established as a primary, an intermediate, a secondary, or a composite school, and the notice establishing it must specify what classification of school it is.
A notice under subsection (1) establishing a school may specify the year levels for which education may be given at the school and may provide for different year levels to be phased in over a specified period or periods.
A notice under subsection (1) establishing a primary school may designate the school as a contributing school.
Despite clauses 3 and 4 of Schedule 22, the Minister may approve an alternative constitution when establishing a school.
This section is subject to section 209 (which sets out consultation requirements).
191 Single-sex schools
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, declare a State school to be a single-sex school or a co-educational school.
A declaration under subsection (1) takes effect on the day that is 5 months after the first day of August after the notice is published.
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, limit, in relation to a specified single-sex school,—
in the case of a boys' school, the number of girls who may enrol at it, or the proportion of the total roll of the school that may be girls:
in the case of a girls' school, the number of boys who may enrol at it, or the proportion of the total roll of the school that may be boys.
In setting limits under subsection (3), the Minister must have regard to the necessity of maintaining the single-sex nature of the school.
Subsection (1) is subject to section 209 (which sets out consultation requirements).
192 Names of State schools
The name of a State school established after 31 December 1989 must be the name specified in the notice establishing it.
The name of a State school whose classification has been changed under section 198 must be the name specified in the last notice changing its classification.
The name of a State school established before 1 January 1990 must be the name it had on 1 December 1989.
However, the board of the State school may, by resolution and with the Secretary's written consent, change the school's name.
The Secretary may not withhold consent under subsection (4) unless satisfied that the proposed new or amended name is inappropriate.
193 Normal schools, etc
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette,—
designate 1 or more specified primary schools as normal or model schools for providers of pre-service teacher education:
designate within a specified primary school a normal or model school, or model class, for providers of pre-service teacher education:
revoke any designation under this section.
Subsection (1) does not apply to a State integrated school.
Every school that was on 31 December 1989 designated a normal or model school under section 72 of the Education Act 1964 must be treated as having been designated a normal school under this section.
Every normal or model school, or model class, that was on 31 December 1989 designated within a primary school under section 72 of the Education Act 1964 must be treated as having been designated under this Act.
Subsection (1) is subject to section 209 (which sets out consultation requirements).
194 Contributing schools
The Minister must determine which primary schools are to be or cease to be contributing schools.
If the Minister determines that a primary school is to be or cease to be a contributing school, the Minister must give the school’s board written notice of the determination.
While a primary school is a contributing school, its board must limit the education given at the school to students enrolled in year levels—
not higher than year 6; and
if the Minister allows in a notice given under subsection (2), not higher than year 7.
However, if satisfied that there are enrolled at a contributing school students whose education at the school is wholly or in part bilingual, the Minister may, by written notice to the board specifying the languages concerned, permit the board to give education to those students in accordance with conditions specified in the notice.
Every school that was on 31 December 1989 a contributing school of any other school under section 74 of the Education Act 1964 must be treated as having been designated under subsection (1).
This section applies to a school designated as a contributing school under section 190(5) as if the school had become a contributing school under subsection (1) and the Minister had given the school's board the appropriate notice under subsection (2).
In determining that a school should be or should cease to be a contributing school, the Minister may provide for education at specified year levels to be phased in or phased out over a specified period or specified periods.
Subsection (1) is subject to section 209 (which sets out consultation requirements).
195 Provision of education at composite schools
The Minister may, by written notice to the board of a composite school, require the board to provide education for the year levels specified in the notice, and if the Minister does so, the board must provide the education at the school accordingly.
Subsection (1) is subject to section 209 (which sets out consultation requirements).
196 Distance schools
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette,—
when establishing the school, or at any other time, designate a State school that is not a State integrated school as a distance school; or
at any time, remove a designation under paragraph (a).
However, the Minister may not designate a school as a distance school, or cancel a designation as a distance school, without having regard to the education of people unable conveniently to receive tuition from a suitable State school that is not a distance school.
Subject to clauses 8(7) and 12 of Schedule 23 and any regulations made under this Act specifying when a board member’s office becomes vacant, if an existing State school is designated as a distance school, its board must continue in office as constituted until a day specified by the Minister in the notice in the Gazette under section 122(1) determining the composition of its board, but must go out of office on that day.
With the consent of the Minister, a distance school may provide early childhood education.
A distance school may deliver education through any medium, including digital technology.
The school that was, immediately before the commencement of the Education Amendment Act 1989, a correspondence school is to be treated as having been—
established under section 190 as a composite school; and
designated under subsection (1) as a distance school.
197 Specialist schools and special services
The Minister may, having regard to the provision of special education in any locality or localities,—
establish a specialist school:
establish, or authorise the establishment of, any special service.
The Minister may disestablish a specialist school or special service established under subsection (1) if the Minister—
is dissatisfied with the manner in which the school or service is being conducted; or
considers that sufficient provision is made by another similarly established school or service, or by any other school or class in or reasonably near to the same locality.
198 Minister may change classification of school
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, do any of the following:
declare a composite school to be a primary, an intermediate, or a secondary school:
declare a primary, an intermediate, or a secondary school to be a composite school:
declare an intermediate school to be a primary or secondary school:
declare a primary or secondary school to be an intermediate school.
Subsection (1) does not apply to a State integrated school.
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, specify the year levels for which education must be given at a school (whether it is an existing school or a school whose classification has been changed under subsection (1)) and provide for year levels to be phased in over a specified period or periods.
A decision to change the classification of a school under subsection (1) or to specify year levels (and, if relevant, to provide for the phasing in of these) under subsection (3) is in the Minister’s absolute discretion.
A notice under subsection (1) must specify a day (no earlier than the end of the term after the term during which the notice is published) on which it is to take effect, and the school becomes a school of the classification concerned, and ceases to be a school of the classification it was, on the day specified.
Subject to clauses 8(7) and 12 of Schedule 23 and any regulations made under this Act specifying when a board member’s office ends or becomes vacant, if an existing State school becomes a school of a different classification, its board continues in office as constituted until the close of the seventh day after the first election following its change of classification.
Subsections (1) and (3) are subject to section 209 (which sets out consultation requirements).
199 Minister may close State school
This section applies if, after consulting the board of a State school, the Minister is satisfied that the school should be closed.
Before making a decision under subsection (1), the Minister may, by written notice to the board, ask the board if it has any arguments in favour of the school staying open.
The Minister may, after considering all arguments (if any) received from the board within 28 days after it received notice under subsection (2), by notice in the Gazette specifying a day on which the school is to close, close the school, and the school ceases to be established on the day specified.
If the board of a State school advises or indicates to the Minister in writing that it agrees to or does not oppose the proposed closure of the school, the Minister may (despite anything in subsection (2) or (3) and regardless of whether the Minister has formally begun or completed the necessary consultation under subsection (1)) close the school by notice under subsection (3) on or after receiving the board's written advice.
A decision to close a school under subsection (3) is in the Minister’s absolute discretion.
If a school is closed under this section,—
its board must be treated as having been dissolved; and
all assets, liabilities, and debts that the board had immediately before dissolution must be treated as assets, liabilities, and debts of the Minister.
Without limiting subsection (6)﻿(b), the following provisions apply to property that was, immediately before dissolution, held by the board in trust for the benefit of the school:
the Minister may apply to Public Trust to devise a scheme to modify the trust for the benefit of another school:
if the Minister applies under paragraph (a), section 208(2) to (8) applies with any necessary modifications (as if the property were property to which that section applies).
A school that has been closed may not be reopened, except by being established again under this Act.
This section is subject to section 209 (which sets out consultation requirements) and clause 22 of Schedule 6.
200 Minister may designate and redesignate schools
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, after consultation with the board of the affected school, do any of the following:
designate a State school that is not a Kura Kaupapa Māori or a designated character school as a Kura Kaupapa Māori or a designated character school:
remove the designation of a Kura Kaupapa Māori and redesignate it as a designated character school:
remove the designation of a designated character school and redesignate it as a Kura Kaupapa Māori:
remove the designation of a Kura Kaupapa Māori or a designated character school while keeping the school established as a State school.
The designation or removal of a designation of a school takes effect on the date specified in the notice under subsection (1) and, on and from that date, section 204—
applies to a school that is designated as a Kura Kaupapa Māori or as a designated character school; and
ceases to apply to a school that has had its designation as a Kura Kaupapa Māori or a designated character school removed.
A notice under subsection (1) may prescribe a new name for the redesignated school.
Section 204 applies in respect of a school that is, or is to be, redesignated as a Kura Kaupapa Māori in the same way as it would apply if the school were being established as a Kura Kaupapa Māori.
No school may have its designation as a Kura Kaupapa Māori removed unless the Minister has first consulted with te kaitiaki o Te Aho Matua.
No State school may be designated as a Kura Kaupapa Māori unless the Minister has first consulted with te kaitiaki o Te Aho Matua on the ability of the school to operate in accordance with Te Aho Matua.
Section 204 applies in respect of a school that is, or is to be, redesignated as a designated character school in the same way as it would apply if the school were being established as a designated character school.
201 Kura Kaupapa Māori
When establishing a State school, the Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, designate the school as a Kura Kaupapa Māori.
The Minister may, in the Minister’s absolute discretion, refuse to establish a school as Kura Kaupapa Māori.
The Minister may not establish a school under this section unless satisfied that—
te reo Māori is to be the main language of instruction at the school:
the school is to operate in accordance with Te Aho Matua:
it is desirable for students whose parents want them to do so to get such an education:
students at the school are to receive an education of a kind that differs significantly from the education they would receive at an ordinary State school.
A Kura Kaupapa Māori may have other special characteristics that give it a particular character (its special characteristics).
The Minister may not establish a State school as a Kura Kaupapa Māori unless the Minister has first consulted te kaitiaki o Te Aho Matua on the ability of the school to operate in accordance with Te Aho Matua.
A notice under subsection (1) must—
specify the name of the school, which must at all times begin with the words “Te Kura Kaupapa Māori o”; and
state that the school will operate in accordance with Te Aho Matua; and
summarise any special characteristics of the school; and
specify the constitution of the board of the school.
After consultation with the board, the Minister may from time to time, by notice in the Gazette, amend the name of the school (but not so as to omit the words “Te Kura Kaupapa Māori o”), its special characteristics, or the constitution of the board.
The board of a school established under this section must ensure that—
te reo Māori is the principal language of instruction at the school; and
the school operates in accordance with Te Aho Matua.
The board may refuse to enrol any person whose parents do not accept that the school operates in accordance with Te Aho Matua.
The Secretary must, by written notice to a Kura Kaupapa Māori, fix a maximum roll of the school, and the board must ensure that the number of students enrolled at the school is not more than the maximum roll.
Except as provided in this section, clause 16 of Schedule 20, and any regulations made under this Act regarding enrolment schemes for Kura Kaupapa Māori, this Act applies to every Kura Kaupapa Māori as if it were not a Kura Kaupapa Māori.
202 Te Aho Matua
The official version of Te Aho Matua is the statement (including any gazetted amendments) in te reo Māori that is—
prepared by te kaitiaki o Te Aho Matua; and
published in the Gazette under the authority of the Minister.
The Minister may authorise the reprinting of all, or the amendment of any part, of Te Aho Matua in the Gazette, but only if asked to do so by te kaitiaki o Te Aho Matua.
When all, or an amendment to any part, of Te Aho Matua is published in the Gazette, the Minister must ensure that an explanation in English of Te Aho Matua or of the amendment is published in the same Gazette.
The explanation must be one that te kaitiaki o Te Aho Matua has approved as being an accurate interpretation of the meaning of the Māori text.
203 Protection of term Kura Kaupapa Māori
A registered school may not use the term Kura Kaupapa Māori in its name unless the school is established under section 201.
Subsection (1) does not apply to a registered school that, immediately before the commencement of this section, is not established under section 201 but uses the term Kura Kaupapa Māori in its name.
204 Designated character schools
When establishing a State school, the Minister may designate the school as a designated character school in accordance with this section and section 205.
The Minister may, in the Minister’s absolute discretion, refuse to establish a designated character school.
The Minister may not establish a school as a designated character school unless satisfied that, if the school is established,—
the school is to have a character that is in some specific way or ways different from the character of ordinary State schools (its different character):
it is desirable for students whose parents want them to do so to get such an education:
students at the school are to receive an education of a kind that differs significantly from the education they would receive at an ordinary State school.
The board of a designated character school must ensure that—
the aims, purposes, and objectives of the school’s different character are set out in the school’s strategic plan; and
the school operates consistently with its different character.
A board of a designated character school may refuse to enrol students whose parents do not accept that the school operates consistently with its different character.
The Secretary must, by written notice to a designated character school, fix a maximum roll of the school, and the board must ensure that the number of students enrolled at the school is not more than the maximum roll.
Except as provided in this section, clause 16 of Schedule 20, and any regulations made under this Act regarding enrolment schemes for designated character schools, this Act applies to every designated character school as if it were not a designated character school.
205 Process for establishing designated character schools
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, when establishing a State school, designate the school as a designated character school.
Before doing so, the Minister must be satisfied of the matters in section 204(3).
The notice establishing a designated character school must—
describe the different character of the school (as defined in section 204(3)﻿(a)):
state the constitution of the school’s board.
The notice establishing a designated character school may also name a body that has a special affiliation with the school or has responsibility for the different character of the school.
The Minister may, after consultation with the board of a designated character school, by notice in the Gazette,—
amend the description of the different character of the school:
name a body that has a special affiliation with the school or has responsibility for the different character of the school:
amend the statement of the constitution of the board.
The Minister must consult any body named under subsection (4) or (5)﻿(b) before amending the description of the school’s different character.
206 Minister may merge schools
The Minister may, by notice in the Gazette, merge 1 or more State schools that are not State integrated schools with another State school that is not a State integrated school, if the Minister is satisfied that—
each board of a school concerned has made reasonable efforts to consult the parents of students (other than adult students) enrolled full-time at the school about the proposed merger; and
the consultation that has taken place has been adequate in all the circumstances; and
the creation of a single school by the merger is appropriate in the circumstances.
A decision to merge a school under subsection (1) is in the Minister’s absolute discretion.
A notice under subsection (1) takes effect on a day (no earlier than the end of the term after the term during which the notice is published) specified in the notice and has effect as follows:
the merging schools are part of the continuing school:
if the continuing school and each merging school are not already administered by a single board,—
the board of each merging school is dissolved; and
all rights, assets, liabilities, and debts of each merging school are vested in the board of the continuing school:
the continuing school is a school of the classification specified in the notice and provides education for the student year levels specified in the notice.
A notice under subsection (1) does not affect the name of the continuing school.
Before a notice under subsection (1) takes effect, the Minister must give notice in the Gazette of whether,—
during the period between a date specified in the notice and the date on which new board members take office following an election, the board of the continuing school is to be—
the board of the continuing school plus co-opted board members representing each merging school; or
a board appointed by the Minister; or
the board of the continuing school is to have an alternative constitution approved under clause 4 of Schedule 22.
This section is subject to sections 207 and 209.
207 Restrictions on mergers in certain cases
A Kura Kaupapa Māori established after the commencement of the Education (Te Aho Matua) Amendment Act 1999 may not merge or be merged with another Kura Kaupapa Māori or other school unless the schools involved in the merger—
operate in accordance with Te Aho Matua; and
use te reo Māori as the principal language of instruction.
Any other Kura Kaupapa Māori may merge with another school only if both schools use te reo Māori as the principal language of instruction and have the same aims, purposes, and objectives.
A designated character school may not merge or be merged with another designated character or other school unless the schools involved in the merger have the same aims, purposes, and objectives, being the aims, purposes, and objectives that constitute the way in which the character of each school is different from the character of ordinary State schools.
208 Property held in trust
This section applies to any property that, immediately before 2 or more schools merged under section 206, was held in trust—
by the board of 1 (or more) of those schools; or
by any other person or body for the benefit of—
1 (or more) of those schools; or
the students or former students of 1 (or more) of those schools.
The property continues to be subject to the trust concerned, but the person or body in whom it is vested may apply to Public Trust to devise a scheme to modify the trust in light of the merger of the schools concerned.
If satisfied that adequate arrangements have been made to meet the costs of doing so, Public Trust must, in consultation with the board concerned, devise a scheme to modify any trust in respect of which Public Trust has received an application under this section and notify the Solicitor-General of the scheme.
If Public Trust notifies the Solicitor-General of a scheme, the Solicitor-General may, by written notice to Public Trust,—
approve the scheme (as originally notified or with any amendments agreed by Public Trust after consultation with the board concerned); or
suggest amendments to it; or
direct that it should not proceed.
If the Solicitor-General directs that it should not proceed, the matter may be dealt with under the Charitable Trusts Act 1957.
If, within 90 days of being notified of a scheme, the Solicitor-General does not suggest amendments to it or direct that it should not proceed, the Solicitor-General is to be treated as having approved the scheme.
If the Solicitor-General approves a scheme notified under this section, the trust concerned has effect accordingly.
The Solicitor-General may not approve a scheme under this section unless satisfied that—
it modifies the trust concerned so as best to give effect to the intentions of the testator, settlor, or other person or body by whom or which the trust was established; and
subject to paragraph (a), it effects the minimum change necessary to enable the trust to operate successfully in light of the merger of the schools concerned.
209 Consultation
The Minister must consult the board of the State school concerned before—
declaring a school to be a single-sex school or a co-educational school under section 191(1):
setting limits by notice under section 191(3) on the number of girls who may attend a boys' school or boys who may attend a girls' school:
designating a primary school as a normal or model school under section 193(1)﻿(a):
designating a normal or model school or a model class within a primary school under section 193(1)﻿(b):
revoking a designation under section 193(1)﻿(c):
determining under section 194(1) that a particular school is to be a contributing school:
limiting the education given at a composite school under section 195:
changing the classification of a school under section 198(1).
The Minister must consult the boards of all the State schools whose rolls might, in the Minister’s opinion, be affected before—
establishing a school under section 190(1):
declaring a school to be a single-sex school or a co-educational school under section 191(1):
determining under section 194(1) that a primary school is to be or cease to be a contributing school:
limiting the education given at a composite school under section 195:
changing the classification of a school under section 198(1):
closing a school under section 199:
redesignating, or removing a designation from, a school under section 200:
merging a school or schools with another under section 206.
Subsection (2)﻿(f) and (h) does not apply if the relevant boards have already been consulted on a closure or merger option as part of a review of the provision of schooling in a particular area.
210 Community education forums
The Minister may appoint a person to—
convene within a specified area public meetings relating to any action the Minister is considering in respect of State schools; and
advise the Minister on the views expressed at the meetings.
The Minister may take, or refuse to take, the action without—
giving the person reasonable time to convene the meetings and advise the Minister; and
considering any advice the person gives the Minister in that time.
211 State integrated schools part of State system
Subject to subsection (2),—
on integration, a State integrated school becomes part of the State system of education in New Zealand; and
this Act and regulations made under this Act apply to a State integrated school.
The enactments applied to State integrated schools by subsection (1)﻿(b) are subject to Schedule 6.
212 Application to negotiate integration
The proprietor of a private school, and any person who proposes to establish a school with the intention that it become a State integrated school, may apply to the Minister to enter into negotiations for integration under Schedule 6.
 Subpart 6A — Charter schools/kura hourua
 Authorisation Board
212A Authorisation Board established
This section establishes the Charter Schools Authorisation Board.
The Authorisation Board is a body corporate with perpetual succession and is capable of—
holding real and personal property; and
suing and being sued; and
otherwise doing and suffering all that bodies corporate may do and suffer.
212B Membership of Authorisation Board
The Authorisation Board consists of at least 5, but not more than 9, members appointed by the Minister by notice in the Gazette.
In appointing members, the Minister must have regard to the knowledge, skills, and experience necessary to carry out the Authorisation Board’s functions, including in the following areas:
education:
governance:
leadership:
financial management:
audit and performance management:
legal and property management:
procurement and contracting:
engagement with Māori, Pacific people, and other stakeholders.
212C Functions and powers of Authorisation Board
The functions of the Authorisation Board are to—
decide on applications by proposed sponsors to operate charter schools:
oversee the performance of charter schools:
provide advice to the Minister on the operation of the charter school model:
provide any other advice or oversight that the Minister requests in writing:
decide whether to apply an intervention referred to in section 212ZF to a sponsor:
perform any other functions conferred on it by this Act or any other legislation.
The Authorisation Board may exercise its powers only for the purpose of performing its functions.
Schedule 6A sets out further provisions relating to the Authorisation Board.
212D Application of Public Service Act 2020 to Authorisation Board
Sections 12 and 17 to 20 of the Public Service Act 2020 apply, with any necessary modifications, to the Authorisation Board as if it were a Crown agent.
212E Application of Crown Entities Act 2004 to Authorisation Board
The provisions of the Crown Entities Act 2004 set out in Schedule 6B apply, with any necessary modifications, to the Authorisation Board as if it were a Crown agent.
 Application to operate charter school
212F Application for approval to operate charter schools
The following may apply to the Authorisation Board for approval to operate a charter school:
a body corporate, corporation sole, limited partnership, institution, or related entity (a proposed sponsor); or
in the case of a converting school,—
the school’s board with the support of the proposed sponsor; or
1 or more members of the school community with the support of the proposed sponsor.
The application must—
be in the approved form (if any); and
be accompanied by—
evidence or information specified in the approved form; and
the prescribed fee (if any).
The Authorisation Board may require the applicant to provide further information to support the application.
The chief executive of the Agency may approve forms for the purposes of this section.
212G Minister may direct board to apply to convert State school to charter school
The Minister may, in the Minister’s absolute discretion and after considering whether it is appropriate in all the circumstances, direct the board of a State school to make an application to the Authorisation Board to convert the State school to a charter school.
Before issuing a direction under subsection (1), the Minister must first seek the views of the Authorisation Board about whether the State school would be appropriate to convert to a charter school.
A direction issued by the Minister must specify the proposed sponsor of the charter school.
The board must make an application to the Authorisation Board with the support of the proposed sponsor named in the direction as soon as practicable after receiving the direction.
Sections 212F, 212I, 212J, and 212K apply, with any necessary modifications, to an application made under this section.
212H Restriction on applications and directions
A specialist school may not apply under section 212F for approval to operate a charter school.
The Minister may not issue a direction under section 212G to the board of any of the following State schools:
a specialist school:
a State integrated school:
a distance school:
a Kura Kaupapa Māori:
a designated character school.
 Approval of sponsor
212I Authorisation Board may approve sponsor
The Authorisation Board may approve a sponsor to operate 1 or more charter schools if satisfied that the proposed sponsor and every governing member of the proposed sponsor are fit and proper persons (after considering the criteria set out in section 212J).
When deciding whether to approve a proposed sponsor, the Authorisation Board must take into account the following matters:
the focus of the proposed charter school:
the capability of the proposed sponsor, including—
its experience and knowledge of the New Zealand schooling system; and
its financial and governance capability:
the standard of tuition to be provided by the proposed charter school, including—
the mode of curriculum delivery; and
the regularity of instruction:
the financial implications for the Crown if the proposed charter school is established:
whether the purpose in section 32(e) would be achieved if the proposed charter school is established:
the level of support for the proposed charter school from the community in which it is proposed to be established:
any other matters that the Authorisation Board considers relevant.
The Authorisation Board must seek the views of the Secretary and the Chief Review Officer on the matters set out in subsections (1) and (2).
In the case of a converting school, the Authorisation Board must also take into account the following matters:
the performance of the State school:
the level of support from the school community, school staff, and students for the proposed sponsor:
the level of support from the school community, school staff, and students for the proposed conversion of the school to a charter school.
For the purpose of ascertaining the matters in subsection (4), the Authorisation Board—
must consult the school board, school community, school staff, and students; and
may consult any other person it thinks fit.
In the case of a sponsor proposing to enter into a multi-school contract, the Authorisation Board must also take into account the following matters:
the overall operating model of the schools the sponsor proposes to operate, including how the sponsor intends to allocate resources between them:
if applicable, how each school that the sponsor is currently operating is performing.
The Authorisation Board may, with the agreement of the sponsor, amend an existing approval to—
add 1 or more charter schools; or
remove 1 or more charter schools.
212J Criteria for assessing whether proposed sponsor and governing members are fit and proper persons
In assessing whether a proposed sponsor is a fit and proper person to operate a charter school, the Authorisation Board must take into account the following matters:
whether the proposed sponsor has been convicted of any offence involving fraud or any crime involving dishonesty:
whether the proposed sponsor owes or has owed money to the Crown, including in respect of bodies the proposed sponsor has been involved in managing:
whether an intervention has been applied in relation to another charter school which the proposed sponsor is or was operating:
whether the proposed sponsor committed a serious or repeated breach of the sponsor’s statutory duties in relation to another charter school:
any other matters that the Authorisation Board considers relevant.
In assessing whether a governing member of a proposed sponsor is a fit and proper person to operate a charter school, the Authorisation Board must take into account the following matters:
whether the person has been convicted of serious criminal activity (as defined in section 10(1)):
whether the person has been—
adjudicated bankrupt under the Insolvency Act 2006:
prohibited from being a director or promoter of, or being concerned or taking part in the management of,—
a company under the Companies Act 1993; or
any other body corporate:
whether an intervention has been applied in relation to another charter school of which the person is or was a governing member of the sponsor:
whether the person committed a serious or repeated breach of the person’s statutory duties as a governing member of the sponsor:
any other matters that the Authorisation Board considers relevant.
For the purposes of subsection (2), if the proposed sponsor is—
a company, the assessment applies to its directors:
an incorporated society or an incorporated trust board, the assessment applies to its governing members:
another body corporate, corporation sole, institution, or its related entity, the assessment applies to its governing members:
a limited partnership, the assessment applies to its partners as defined in the Limited Partnerships Act 2008.
212K Conditions on approval
If the school to which an approval relates is a converting school, the following conditions apply to the approval:
all students currently enrolled at the State school who wish to attend the charter school must be allowed to do so:
if the State school is a designated character school, a Kura Kaupapa Māori, or a State integrated school, the character of the school must be maintained:
if the State school is an ordinary State school, it must not provide education with a special character or that is different from the character of ordinary State schools.
When granting an approval of a sponsor, the Authorisation Board may impose any other conditions on the approval as it thinks fit, including conditions relating to the maximum number of international students that may be enrolled at the charter school.
The Authorisation Board may—
impose new conditions on the approval; or
amend or revoke any existing conditions.
However, the Authorisation Board may not amend or remove the conditions specified in subsection (1).
 Charter school contracts
212L Charter school contracts
The Agency may, in the name and on behalf of the Crown, enter into a contract with a sponsor that allows the sponsor to operate 1 or more charter schools.
If the Agency is unable to conclude a contract with the sponsor within a reasonable time, it must refer the matter back to the Authorisation Board for further consideration.
When negotiating a charter school contract, the Agency must have regard to the available budget for charter schools.
The charter school contract must be for a fixed term.
The charter school contract must set out, or provide for, the following matters:
the physical capacity of the school premises:
the conditions that apply to the approval of the sponsor under section 212K:
whether the sponsor intends to operate the charter school for profit or not-for-profit:
the performance targets and outcomes that the sponsor must meet in relation to the operation of the school:
